  Assignment context                                                            
  Sefer turns raw shipping documents into US customs entries ready to file. The 
  assignment is to build a simple web application that accepts shipment         
  documents and produces a NetCHB-ready customs entry, along with missing       
  information and recommended next steps.                                       
  Sample shipment documents is https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/17tYnBbvy 
  4qjzrn3773L6Kh_4yBjV1A7p?usp=drive_link                                       
  The application should extract relevant information from commercial invoices, 
  packing lists, and bills of lading identify missing, conflicting, or          
  uncertain values and generate the appropriate entry XML based on verified     
  NetCHB requirements.                                                          
  Deliverables are:                                                             
  1. A GitHub repository with a TypeScript frontend and backend, plus a README. 
  2. XML generated for the provided sample shipment.                            
  3. The complete AI chat history.                                              
  4. A one-page write-up explaining the approach.                               
                                                                                
  Requirements for CLAUDE.md :                                                  
  explain the project and deliverables , make beginner friendly explanation,    
  structure responses and step by step work. Before coding first focus on       
  research and planning. Find out official and trustable resources for NetCHB   
  requirements. Never invent XML tags, schemas, endpoints, customs rules,       
  extracted values, or unsupported capabilities. Clearly distinguish verified   
  facts, assumptions, unresolved questions  and implementation decisions.Never  
  guess the shipment detail, take conformation where ever needed. Keep the      
  solution simple, clean and basic UI and one which wcan be completed in 48 hrs 
  (24 hr commitment). Require testing against the provided shipment and         
  consideration of unseen shipments.Do not initialize Git, create a GitHub      
  repository, publish code or push anything without my explicit approval.Dont   
  start implementation and codeing until i approve the research work and        
  proposed plan. Explain important technical decisions in language I can        
  understand and defend/ explain when asked in interview. Preserve the actual   
  AI conversation for submission; do not clear or replace the conversation as a 
  substitute for maintaining its history.                                       
                                                                                
  Requirements for prompt.md:                                                   
  Include the assignment context, sample document link, requirements and        
  deliverables.                                                                 
  i will use a defined phase wise workflow:                                     
  Phase 1: Inspect the environment and obtain the sample documents.             
  Phase 2: Research official NetCHB documentation and determine verified XML    
  requirements.                                                                 
  Phase 3: Analyze the shipment documents and map source fields to required     
  entry fields.                                                                 
  Phase 4: Propose the architecture, extraction approach, validation strategy,  
  and project plan.                                                             
  Phase 5: Implement the approved plan incrementally.                           
  Phase 6: Test extraction, missing/conflicting information handling, and XML   
  validity.                                                                     
  Phase 7: Generate the sample shipment XML and prepare the README, one-page    
  write-up, and submission checklist.                                           
  Include  checkpoints requiring my approval before moving from research to     
  implementation and before any GitHub publication.Include a research log       
  format recording the question, source, evidence, conclusion and remaining     
  uncertainty.Include an extraction validation checklist and a final            
  deliverables checklist.State that requirements not verified from reliable     
  evidence must remain explicitly unresolved rather than being presented as     
  facts.                                                                        
                                                                                
  IMPORTANT CONSTRAINTS                                                         
  1. This is to only build documentation, dont build or write any code or       
  implement

  CLAUDE.md: rules for the AI assistant

  Section: 1. Project Overview
  What it covers: Sefer, customs entries, the three document types and NetCHB in

  plain language; the deliverables and the 24-hour budget
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: 2. Golden Rules
  What it covers: No code before you approve the research and plan; no Git or
  GitHub actions without your approval; never invent tags, schemas, endpoints,
  rules or values; ask before using any unclear shipment detail; keep the full
  conversation
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: 3. Research Before Coding
  What it covers: Research comes first, with three trust levels for sources
  (official NetCHB and CBP sources are the top level)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: 4. Response Style
  What it covers: Structured, step-by-step answers; important decisions
  explained
  as What / Why / Alternatives / Trade-offs so you can defend them in an
  interview
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: 5. Solution Principles
  What it covers: Keep it simple, TypeScript on both ends, and show missing,
  conflicting and uncertain values separately in the app
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: 6. Testing
  What it covers: Test on the sample shipment, design for unseen shipments, and
  check the XML
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: 7–8. Checkpoints and Workspace
  What it covers: Summary of approval gates, plus a warning about the
  home-directory Git repository

  Every claim must carry one of four labels: ✅ Verified Fact, 🔶 Assumption, ❓
  Unresolved Question or 🛠️ Implementation Decision.

  prompt.md: assignment brief and workflow

  - Brief: the assignment context, the sample document link, 10 numbered
    requirements (R1–R10) and the 4 deliverables (D1–D4).
  - Unverified requirements: anything not backed by reliable evidence stays
    marked UNRESOLVED and is never presented as fact.
  - Phases 1–7: each phase has a goal, numbered steps and an expected output.
  - Approval gates:
    - A: approve the research, after Phase 3.
    - B: approve the plan, before any code.
    - C: approve before running git init.
    - D: approve before publishing to GitHub.
    - Ongoing: confirm any shipment value I'm unsure of.
  - Research log format: question, source, trust level of the source, evidence,
    conclusion and what remains uncertain.
  - Extraction checklist: covers document handling, field extraction, issue
    detection, the XML output and how well it handles new shipments.
  - Final deliverables checklist: one section per deliverable, plus a final
    review.

  Next step

  I'm ready to start Phase 1: check the tools and get the sample documents. I
  may not be able to download from the Google Drive link automatically. If so,
  I'll ask you to download the files into a folder you choose, for example
  sample-docs/ inside the project.

  Do you want me to start Phase 1, or would you like changes to either file
  first?

● Phase 2 and Phase 3: consolidated findings (stopped at Checkpoint A)

  Summary

  - The NetCHB XML format is publicly documented, which is good news. NetCHB
    hosts its official schema (entry.xsd), a sample file and a page explaining
    its responses. Those three let us build the entry XML from real, cited
    definitions instead of guessing.
  - The sample shipment has many deliberate problems. I found 12 conflicts or 
    issues that need your confirmation. The biggest: a maths error on the
    invoice, a quantity mismatch, a fibre-content mismatch that changes the
    tariff classification, a gross-weight mismatch, and a corrected container
    number.
  - Some things the schema can't tell us. The live submission endpoint, NetCHB's
    full integration guide (behind a login) and several broker-only values
    aren't publicly available. They stay ❓ unresolved.

  Done so far:
  - Copied the 3 PDFs into sample-docs/ and checked that each copy is
    byte-for-byte identical to the download.
  - All research files are in my temporary scratchpad. The analysis scripts I
    ran there were read-only research tools, not application code.

  Not done: no code, no Git, and no changes to CLAUDE.md or prompt.md.

  ▎ ⚠️ Integrity note: my web searches turned up other candidates' public GitHub
  ▎ repositories for this same assignment. I deliberately did not open or use 
  ▎ them. They're unverified sources, and using them would undermine your 
  ▎ submission.

  Part 1: Phase 2, NetCHB research

  1.1 Research log

  ID: RL-3
  Question: Does NetCHB publish an official XML format for entries?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): netchb.com/xml/entry/entry.xsd
  (https://www.netchb.com/xml/entry/entry.xsd) and data_type.xsd
  (https://www.netchb.com/xml/data/data_type.xsd)
  Trust level: 1: official NetCHB
  Evidence: The schema says it is "the XML Schema for an entry summary XML
  document that will be used to validate any entry submitted to NetChb using
  the entry summary web service." Namespace http://www.netchb.com/xml/entry;
  root element <entry>.
  Conclusion: ✅ Verified: NetCHB accepts entry data as XML defined by
  entry.xsd.
  This confirms the 🔶 assumption written in CLAUDE.md.
  What remains uncertain: None for the structure.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-4
  Question: Is there an official example?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): entry_sample.xml
  (https://www.netchb.com/xml/entry/entry_sample.xml) (I found this file by
  trying the obvious file name on NetCHB's own server; it isn't linked from any
   page)
  Trust level: 1
  Evidence: Its header says: "any entry that is submitted via this webservice
  will be validated against the …/entry.xsd schema." It contains placeholder
  values (port 8888, tariff 1234567890).
  Conclusion: ✅ Verified: an official sample exists and confirms the schema is 
  what NetCHB enforces.
  What remains uncertain: See RL-5.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-5
  Question: Is the sample consistent with the schema?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): I validated the sample against the schema with
  lxml in the scratchpad
  Trust level: 1 (both NetCHB files)
  Evidence: It fails at line 758 ("dea-drug-code: This element is not
  expected").
  It also uses bond type 08 in a spot where the schema's descriptive comment
  mentions 1/2; the schema's actual allowed values are 00, 08 and 09.
  Conclusion: ✅ Verified: the sample and schema have drifted apart. 🛠️ We treat

  entry.xsd as the authority and use the sample only to understand how fields
  are used.
  What remains uncertain: Whether the public schema is NetCHB's current
  production version.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-6
  Question: What happens after an upload?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): entryUploadResponse.html
  (https://www.netchb.com/xml/entry/entryUploadResponse.html)
  Trust level: 1
  Evidence: Operations named: uploadEntry, amendEntry, queryEntryStatus.
  Responses are <accepted>/<rejected>. NetCHB says acceptance "does not mean
  the entry was accepted by customs." The optional <transmit/> tag makes NetCHB
   send the entry to CBP automatically.
  Conclusion: ✅ Verified: an upload only creates the entry in NetCHB. Leaving 
  out <transmit/> means nothing is sent to CBP.
  What remains uncertain: —
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-7
  Question: How exactly is the XML submitted (endpoint, login)?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): The NetCHB service path /main/services/ and the

  /app/xml page
  Trust level: 1
  Evidence: /app/xml is a login page. The entry-upload WSDL (the file that
  defines a web service's endpoint) wasn't found at the paths I tried (HTTP
  404). A different service, JamcoXmlUploadService with uploadXml(username, 
  password, xmlData), is public, but it isn't the entry service.
  Conclusion: ❓ Unresolved: the submission endpoint and authentication.
  What remains uncertain: Needs NetCHB credentials or documentation from Sefer.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-8
  Question: Which fields does the schema itself require?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): entry.xsd (structural analysis)
  Trust level: 1
  Evidence: See §1.2.
  Conclusion: ✅ Verified
  What remains uncertain: —
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-9
  Question: Which fields are required only in some cases?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): Comments in entry_sample.xml and entry.xsd
  Trust level: 1
  Evidence: For example: "port of unlading is required for ocean shipments",
  "vessel-name … required for ocean shipments … maximum of 20 characters",
  "importer-tax-id: Required unless the entry type is 86".
  Conclusion: ✅ Verified as documented by NetCHB. The schema can't enforce
  these
  rules, so our app must check them itself.
  What remains uncertain: These rules may be incomplete; the full guide is
  behind
  a login.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-10
  Question: What is the mode-of-transport code for containerised ocean cargo?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): CBP Form 7501 instructions
  (https://www.cbp.gov/sites/default/files/2026-02/cbp_form_7501.pdf); ACE
  Entry Summary instructions
  (https://www.cbp.gov/sites/default/files/documents/ace_es_instructions_3.pdf)
  Trust level: 1: CBP
  Evidence: "11 – Vessel, container". For modes 10/11 the vessel name and bill
  of
  lading number are mandatory.
  Conclusion: ✅ Verified: 11
  What remains uncertain: —
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-11
  Question: What is the CBP port code for Tacoma?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): CBP: Tacoma, WA – 3002
  (https://www.cbp.gov/about/contact/ports/tacoma-washington-washington-3002)
  Trust level: 1
  Evidence: Port code 3002
  Conclusion: ✅ Verified: 3002
  What remains uncertain: Whether processing port = entry port = unlading port
  is
  the broker's call.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-12
  Question: What is the foreign port code for Cat Lai (NetCHB requires 5
  digits)?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): CBP ACE Appendix F, Schedule K (Aug 2024)
  (https://cbp.gov/sites/default/files/2024-08/ACE%20Appendix%20F%20Schedule%20K
  _21_8_2024_v1_508_0.pdf)
  Trust level: 1
  Evidence: "55224 – Ho Chi Minh City; Saigon". Cat Lai isn't listed separately.
  Conclusion: 🔶 Assumption: Cat Lai (a terminal in Ho Chi Minh City) → 55224.
  What remains uncertain: Needs broker confirmation; a newer appendix may exist.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-13
  Question: How are CIF freight and insurance and buyer-supplied materials
  treated in customs value?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): 19 U.S.C. §1401a
  (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/19/1401a); CBP "Proper Deductions"
  (https://www.cbp.gov/sites/default/files/assets/documents/2016-Apr/icp049_3.pd
  f)
  Trust level: 1
  Evidence: §1401a(b)(4)(A): price excludes costs "incident to the international

  shipment". §1401a(b)(1)(C): the value of assists (materials the buyer
  supplies for production) is added. Deductions must be actual costs.
  Conclusion: ✅ Verified rules. How they apply to this shipment is a broker 
  decision (see §2.3).
  What remains uncertain: Whether the invoice freight and insurance figures are
  actual costs.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-14
  Question: Is the 6-digit HS code enough?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): USITC HTS FAQ
  (https://www.usitc.gov/harmonized_tariff_information/frequently_asked_question
  s)
  Trust level: 1
  Evidence: The full 10-digit HTSUS number must be used on entries. The schema
  allows \d{5,10}.
  Conclusion: ✅ Verified: the invoice's 6-digit codes are not sufficient.
  What remains uncertain: The 10-digit codes are a classification decision; we 
  must not invent them.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: RL-15
  Question: How is a manufacturer ID (MID) built?
  Source (accessed 10 Oct 2026): CBP Directive 3550-055
  (https://www.cbp.gov/sites/default/files/documents/3550-055_3.pdf);
  TBT-05-031 (textiles)
  (https://www.cbp.gov/document/rulings/tbt-05-031-manufacturer-identification-c
  ode-mid-requirements-textiles-and-textile)
  Trust level: 1
  Evidence: The MID is built by an algorithm from the manufacturer's name and
  address. For textiles, it uses the entity that performed the operations that
  confer origin.
  Conclusion: ✅ Verified rule source.
  What remains uncertain: ❓ Whether to compute MIDs in the app or ask for them;

  to be decided in Phase 4.

  1.2 Verified XML requirements (from entry.xsd)

  Element order matters: the schema says the children of <entry> "must appear in
  the order specified."

  Level: <entry>
  Required by the schema: entry-no (either user-specified or system-generated),
  header, manifest, invoices
  Ocean or formal-entry conditions (NetCHB comments): —
  Format rules verified in the schema: Namespace http://www.netchb.com/xml/entry
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Level: <header>
  Required by the schema: processing-port, entry-port, entry-date, entry-type
  Ocean or formal-entry conditions (NetCHB comments): importer-tax-id (unless
  type 86); mode-transportation and state-destination (formal); unlading-port,
  vessel-name (≤ 20 characters) and voyage-no (ocean)
  Format rules verified in the schema: Ports: 4 digits. Dates: yyyy-mm-dd. Tax
  ID: patterns such as NN-NNNNNNN[XX]. description ≤ 70 characters.
  gross-weight and charges are whole numbers.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Level: <manifest>/<bill-of-lading>
  Required by the schema: quantity, unit (when a B/L is given)
  Ocean or formal-entry conditions (NetCHB comments): B/L number mandatory for
  modes 10/11 (CBP)
  Format rules verified in the schema: Bill numbers: letters and digits only, ≤
  50. SCAC: 2 or 4 characters. unit ≤ 5 characters.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Level: <invoice>
  Required by the schema: invoice-no, line-items
  Ocean or formal-entry conditions (NetCHB comments): —
  Format rules verified in the schema: ⚠️ invoice-no must match
  [A-Za-z\d\-]{1,17}, so slashes are not  allowed
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Level: <line-item>
  Required by the schema: country-origin, tariffs
  Ocean or formal-entry conditions (NetCHB comments): country-export
  (ocean/air),
  lading-port (ocean, 5 digits)
  Format rules verified in the schema: Country: 2 letters.
  commercial-description
  ≤ 70 characters.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Level: <tariff>
  Required by the schema: tariff-no, value
  Ocean or formal-entry conditions (NetCHB comments): —
  Format rules verified in the schema: Tariff number: 5–10 digits. Value:
  dollars
  with 2 decimals. Units of measure come from an 82-code list (CATAIR Appendix
  C), e.g. CTN.

  1.3 Unresolved after Phase 2

  1. ❓ The submission endpoint and authentication (RL-7).
  2. ❓ Whether the public schema is the current production version (RL-5).
  3. ❓ What unit <gross-weight> uses; the schema doesn't say. The B/L figure is
     in KGS.
  4. ❓ Any NetCHB rules documented only behind the login.

  2.3 Conflicts and issues needing your confirmation (Ongoing rule)

  I've checked all the arithmetic. Each item shows the evidence, not my guess at
  the answer.

  ID: C-1
  Issue: Line 1 amount looks misprinted
  Evidence: 2,400 × 2.85 = 6,840.00, but 6,480.00 is printed. The printed
  subtotal of 21,975.00 only adds up with 6,840 (6,840 + 8,880 + 6,255). With
  6,480 it would be 21,615. FOB 23,235 and CIF 27,270 are both consistent with
  6,840.
  Why it matters: The line value goes into <tariff><value>
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C-2
  Issue: Hoodie quantity
  Evidence: Invoice 1,200 pcs vs PL 1,176 pcs (49 cartons × 24)
  Why it matters: A 24-piece difference; the quantity reported on the entry is
  affected
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C-3
  Issue: Hoodie fibre content
  Evidence: Invoice "60% Cotton 40% Polyester" vs PL "TC 65/35"
  Why it matters: Classification depends on which fibre predominates. The
  invoice
  HS 6110.20 is the cotton heading. 🔶 In the garment trade "TC" usually means
  polyester/cotton, which would make polyester predominant; that's unverified.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C-4
  Issue: Gross weight
  Evidence: B/L 1,930.000 kg vs PL 1,888.00 kg (the PL rows add up correctly;
  the
  PL says "palletized: NO")
  Why it matters: A 42 kg difference; the entry needs one figure
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C-5
  Issue: Container number
  Evidence: Typed OPLU3041728 is crossed out and replaced by a handwritten,
  initialled OPLU3041722. I computed the ISO 6346 check digit (a public
  algorithm that validates container numbers) for OPLU304172: 2.
  Why it matters: ✅ The arithmetic supports the handwritten number. Please
  confirm, because my reading of the handwriting is an interpretation.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C-6
  Issue: Buyer-supplied fabric for W-B220
  Evidence: The invoice note says 1,450 m of fabric, USD 4,180.00, was supplied
  free by the buyer and is "not included in this invoice"
  Why it matters: This is an assist under 19 U.S.C. §1401a(b)(1)(C) (RL-13).
  Adding it and apportioning it is a broker decision.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C-7
  Issue: Origin of the samples
  Evidence: Invoice line 5 and the PL note: made in Bangladesh (SP Garments
  Dhaka). But the invoice header says country of origin "VIETNAM", the
  declaration says "goods are of Vietnam origin", and the carton marks
  (including carton 138) say "MADE IN VIETNAM".
  Why it matters: Line 5 country-origin = BD; possible marking problem; and the
  declaration contradicts line 5
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C-8
  Issue: Samples' value
  Evidence: "FOC – no commercial value", but "USD 1.00/pc for customs purpose";
  excluded from the invoice total
  Why it matters: Whether to declare $24.00 or another value is a broker
  decision
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C-9
  Issue: Who are the importer and ultimate consignee?
  Evidence: The invoice sells to Northbound Outfitters; the B/L consignee is
  Cascade Fulfillment (a warehouse, "for account of Northbound")
  Why it matters: This determines importer-tax-id and ultimate-consignee; no tax

  IDs appear anywhere
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C-10
  Issue: Invoice number format
  Evidence: KBAS/NB/26-0912 contains /; the schema allows only letters, digits
  and -
  Why it matters: 🛠️ It must be transformed (e.g. KBASNB26-0912), and that needs

  your or the broker's approval
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C-11
  Issue: Invoice date
  Evidence: 12/09/2026 could be 12 Sep or 9 Dec. Context (PL dated 20 Sep, ETD
  24
  Sep) suggests 12 Sep.
  Why it matters: I won't assume; please confirm
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ID: C-12
  Issue: CIF deductions
  Evidence: CIF 27,270.00 includes ocean freight 3,850.00 and insurance 185.00
  Why it matters: Under RL-13 these are deductible if they're actual costs.
  Excluding them gives FOB 23,235.00 before the C-1, C-6 and C-8 adjustments.

  Minor notes, not blocking:
  - The B/L shows "CFS / DOOR" alongside "shipper's load, stow and count".
  - "TELEX RELEASED" is stamped 25-SEP-2026.
  - The ISF was filed by the "importer/agent", and the broker is "TBA".

  2.4 Assessment of extraction options for the scanned PDFs (nothing installed)

  Option: A. Local OCR (e.g. Tesseract, which has a JavaScript port)
  How it works: Turns each page into an image and recognises the characters
  Strengths: Free, works offline, no API key
  Weaknesses on these documents: Gives plain text with no field structure. 🔶
  Likely to struggle with the overlapping stamps, the handwritten container
  number and table layouts. Still needs a separate step to find fields.
  Setup and time: Medium: needs a PDF-to-image step
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: B. Cloud document OCR (AWS Textract, Google Document AI, Azure
  Document
  Intelligence)
  How it works: Managed OCR that also recognises tables and forms
  Strengths: Detects tables and key-value pairs
  Weaknesses on these documents: Needs a cloud account and keys; costs money;
  still needs mapping to our fields
  Setup and time: Medium to high
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: C. AI vision model (e.g. an LLM that takes PDF or image input and
  returns structured JSON)
  How it works: The model reads the page and fills in a defined set of fields
  Strengths: Handles layout, stamps, handwriting and messy tables in one step;
  easy to adapt to new layouts
  Weaknesses on these documents: Needs an API key and costs money. It can return
   
  wrong values that look plausible, so every field needs a source quote, a
  confidence level, cross-document checks and human confirmation.
  Setup and time: Low to medium
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Option: D. Text layer only (pdftotext / pdf.js)
  How it works: Reads embedded text
  Strengths: Exact, free, deterministic
  Weaknesses on these documents: ✅ Works for the invoice; ✅ verified to return

  nothing for the PL and B/L
  Setup and time: Low

  Preliminary view, to be decided at Checkpoint B (Phase 4): a hybrid approach.
  - Use D wherever a PDF has a text layer.
  - Use C for scanned pages.
  - Put a deterministic validation layer after both. This is plain code, not AI:
    arithmetic checks, cross-document comparisons, checks against the schema's
    formats, and the conditional rules from RL-9.

  Evidence for this view: I was able to read these scans visually in this
  session, which suggests vision models can handle them (🔶). The app's own
  pipeline would still need to be tested separately. Option A is the fallback if
  you want no external API.

● Phase 4: architecture and implementation plan (stopped at Checkpoint B)

  Summary: I've saved the approved research as docs/research-log.md, with all
  sources, evidence, labels, the C-1 to C-12 handling rules, and a prominent
  "not production-verified" notice. Below is a plan that fits the remaining ~20
  working hours.

  machine. (I checked only whether one exists, not its value.) The recommended
  extraction approach for the scanned documents needs one, so this is decision

  ---

  1. Architecture

  mermaid
  flowchart LR
    U[Browser: React UI] -->|upload 1–N PDFs| API[Express API]
    API --> C[1. Classify document type]
    C --> T{Text layer?}
    T -->|yes| X1[2a. Read text layer]
    T -->|no / scanned| X2[2b. Vision extraction]
    X1 --> L[3. Structured field extraction with evidence]
    X2 --> L
    L --> R[4. Reconcile across documents]
    R --> V[5. Validation rules engine]
    V --> S[(Entry draft + issue list)]
    S --> UI2[Review screen]
    UI2 -->|confirmations / broker inputs| B[6. XML builder]
    B --> XSD[7. Local XSD validation against entry.xsd]
    XSD --> D[Download XML + validation report]

  Repository layout (npm workspaces: one repository containing three packages):

  /shared   → TypeScript types + Zod schemas (one definition used by front and 
  back end)
  /server   → Express API: extraction, reconciliation, validation, XML 
  generation
    /schemas/netchb → entry.xsd + data_type.xsd (copied from netchb.com, with 
  source URL and date)
  /web      → React + Vite single-page UI
  /docs     → research-log.md, write-up, generated sample output
  /sample-docs, /fixtures, /tests

  Key decisions (what / why / alternatives / trade-off)

  #: D1
  🛠️ Decision: Express + React (Vite), all TypeScript
  Why: The best-known, best-documented choices, so they're easy to explain in an

  interview; fastest to build
  Alternatives rejected: Next.js (more framework to learn and explain); Fastify
  (fine, but less familiar)
  Trade-off: Not the newest option, but proven
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D2
  🛠️ Decision: Shared Zod schemas
  Why: One definition of "an extracted field" checks data at runtime and gives
  TypeScript types on both sides
  Alternatives rejected: Duplicated interfaces (they drift apart)
  Trade-off: One extra small library
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D3
  🛠️ Decision: Every value carries evidence
  Why: Each field stores {value, sourceDocument, page, verbatimQuote, 
  confidence,
   state}. This is how the app shows conflicts and never invents values.
  Alternatives rejected: Storing bare values (impossible to audit)
  Trade-off: Slightly bigger data model
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D4
  🛠️ Decision: The AI only transcribes; code does all the reasoning
  Why: The model copies what's printed (e.g. "6,480.00" exactly as shown). All
  arithmetic, cross-checks and rules run in deterministic code.
  Alternatives rejected: Letting the AI "fix" or classify values (that would
  hide
  C-1 and C-3)
  Trade-off: More code, but the results are repeatable and testable
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D5
  🛠️ Decision: Generate XML with a builder library; follow the schema's element 
  order
  Why: It escapes special characters correctly, and RL-9 says the order matters
  Alternatives rejected: String templates (escaping bugs)
  Trade-off: —
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D6
  🛠️ Decision: Validate locally against NetCHB's own entry.xsd
  Why: Objective, official check (RL-3)
  Alternatives rejected: Hand-written checks only
  Trade-off: Needs a working XSD validator in Node (see risk R3)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D7
  🛠️ Decision: No submission, no <transmit/>, download only
  Why: Your Checkpoint A decision (RL-6, RL-7)
  Alternatives rejected: —
  Trade-off: Integration not production-verified (stated in the README)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D8
  🛠️ Decision: No persistence
  Why: Uploads are processed in memory; no database
  Alternatives rejected: Database (unneeded for this scope)
  Trade-off: Results are lost on refresh; downloads keep them

  ---

  3. Extraction strategy

  1. Classification: detect whether a file is an invoice, packing list, B/L or
     unknown, from its text or the model's answer. The user can override it in 
     the UI, which matters for unseen documents.
  2. Text-layer PDFs (like the invoice): pull the text deterministically, then
     extract structured fields from it.
  3. Scanned PDFs (packing list, B/L): send the PDF to Claude as a document. The
     API accepts PDFs directly, up to 32 MB per request; ✅ per the Claude API
     reference.
  4. Structured output: use a Zod schema per document type with the SDK's
     structured-output feature. Every field must include a verbatim quote and a
     confidence level. Instructions to the model: transcribe exactly; return
     null if a value isn't present; never infer, correct or classify.
     - ✅ The API's built-in citations can't be combined with structured output,
       so we ask for the quotes as fields instead.
  5. Model: claude-opus-5-5, the current default, at roughly $4 / $20 per
     million input/output tokens. The sample run costs cents. If you'd rather
     use a cheaper model, that's your call.
  6. Without an API key (fallback, decision #2): the app still runs. Text-layer
     PDFs are extracted, and scanned documents show "Extraction unavailable — 
     manual entry required" with editable fields.
     - Local OCR (Tesseract) is an optional extra only if time allows. On these
       stamped, handwritten scans it would 🔶 likely produce low-quality
       results.

  4. Validation rules (deterministic code)

  Category: Format
  Rules: Every schema pattern, length and enumeration (ports \d{4}, invoice-no
  [A-Za-z\d\-]{1,17}, tariff \d{5,10}, vessel-name ≤ 20, …) plus full XSD
  validation
  Source: RL-3, RL-8
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Category: Required
  Rules: Elements required by the schema, plus the documented conditional rules
  (ocean → unlading-port, vessel-name, voyage-no, lading-port, country-export;
  formal → mode-transportation, state-destination; type ≠ 86 → importer-tax-id)
  Source: RL-8, RL-9
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Category: Arithmetic
  Rules: quantity × unit price = line amount; sum of lines = subtotals and
  totals; CIF − freight − insurance = FOB; packing-list rows = packing-list
  totals
  Source: Catches C-1
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Category: Cross-document
  Rules: Same field across documents: quantity per style, fibre content, gross
  weight, carton count, CBM, vessel/voyage, ports, invoice and B/L numbers,
  origin vs carton marks
  Source: Catches C-2, C-3, C-4, C-7
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Category: Identifiers
  Rules: ISO 6346 container check digit; ambiguous date formats (e.g.
  12/09/2026)
  Source: C-5, C-11
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Category: Broker-review flags
  Rules: Wording such as "FOC/NCV", "buyer-supplied/CMT", CIF/CIP terms; 6-digit

  HS codes; MID missing
  Source: C-6, C-8, C-12, RL-14, RL-15

  Issue states (wording as you approved):
  - Missing — broker input required
  - Conflicting source documents — confirmation required
  - Uncertain — confirmation required
  - Provisional — confirmation required (C-5)

  Each issue shows the evidence from every document and a recommended next step.

  5. XML output policy

  - Approved values only: the XML contains only values that are found and
    consistent, confirmed, or entered by a broker. Never placeholders or 
    guesses.
  - When required data is missing: the app produces a DRAFT that is clearly
    marked "NOT READY TO FILE", with a report listing every missing required
    element and every failed XSD check. It never quietly produces an "invalid
    but plausible" file.
  - For the sample shipment (deliverable D2): I expect a draft. It will fail XSD
    validation honestly because the broker-only fields are missing (importer
    tax ID, 10-digit HTS codes, entry date, …). It ships with its validation
    report and issue list. I think that's the correct, defensible result.
  - Original invoice number: always kept in the report. A transformed invoice-no
    is used only if you approve it (decision #5).

  6. Error handling

  ┌─────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │           Failure           │                 Behaviour                 │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Not a PDF, too large        │                                           │
  │ (proposed 20 MB limit),     │ Rejected with a clear message             │
  │ encrypted                   │                                           │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Unknown document type       │ The user picks the type, or the file is   │
  │                             │ ignored with a warning                    │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ AI call fails, times out or │ That document is marked "extraction       │
  │  refuses                    │ failed" with a retry button; the others   │
  │                             │ still process                             │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ AI output fails our schema  │ Treated as a failed extraction; never     │
  │                             │ partially trusted                         │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ A document type is missing  │ Its fields become "Missing" issues;       │
  │ (e.g. no B/L)               │ processing continues                      │
  ├─────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                             │ The rule-based checks still run, and the  │
  │ XSD validator unavailable   │ report states that XSD validation didn't  │
  │                             │ run                                       │
  └─────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Security:
  - The API key stays only in a server .env file, which is git-ignored, never
    sent to the browser and never pasted into chat.
  - Uploads aren't saved to disk.

  7. Testing plan (Phase 6)

  1. Unit tests (Vitest, no API calls): every validation rule, the check-digit
     routine, date-ambiguity detection, the XML builder's element order and
     escaping.
  2. Golden test for the sample: a hand-verified fixture of the sample's
     extracted fields. The test asserts that all 12 issues (C-1 to C-12) are 
     detected and that the draft XML matches the expected output.
  3. XSD tests: a complete synthetic entry made from clearly fake test values,
     only in test fixtures and never in deliverables, must pass entry.xsd; the
     sample draft must fail with exactly the expected missing elements.
  4. Unseen-shipment tests: variations of the fixture covering:
     - a missing B/L
     - a non-USD invoice
     - two invoices
     - an extra line item
     - a clean shipment with no conflicts
     - a container number with a wrong check digit
  5. Live extraction check (runs only if an API key is set): extraction on the
     three sample PDFs, compared field by field with the golden fixture. Results
     are reported honestly.
  6. The extraction validation checklist from prompt.md §8, completed.

  How it behaves

  - Invoice (text-based PDF): every field and all 5 lines are read exactly as
    printed, including the wrong amount 6,480.00.
  - Packing list and bill of lading (scans): the app says "Not read" and offers
    a manual-entry form or a transcription-file import. A transcription is only
    accepted for the PDF it was made from.
  - The two sample scans: I transcribed them by hand from the images, following
    the field mapping you approved. The files, the app and the report all say
    so. Please check them against the PDFs before relying on them.
  - XML: it only contains values the documents agree on, or values you typed or
    explicitly confirmed. Suggested values (port 3002, invoice number
    KBASNB26-0912, container OPLU3041722, and others) stay unused until you
    confirm them. Every gap is marked inside the XML. There is no <transmit/>,
    and the system-generated entry number is off by default.
  - Readiness: the draft only reaches "passes local checks" if XSD validation
    passes, every required field is present, and every blocking issue is
    acknowledged. Even then it says broker review is still needed. A clean test
    shipment I made up does reach that state.

  Known limitations

  - Automatic reading only works for text-based invoices. It was tested on the
    sample's layout and one made-up alternative layout.
  - No OCR or AI vision, so scans always need typing or a transcription file.
  - One bill of lading per entry. No currency conversion. No duty, fee or agency
    (PGA) data.
  - I couldn't look at the UI in a real browser. I checked it by building it,
    type-checking it and running its API calls through the dev server. Please 
    click through it once with npm run dev.
  - Local validation only checks structure. Whether NetCHB or CBP would accept
    the file is untested, because the upload endpoint and login aren't
    available.
  - I added an erratum to docs/research-log.md (§6) rather than changing
    approved findings. The research said line-item commercial-description is
    limited to 70 characters, but the schema has no limit on it. The
    70-character limit applies only to other description fields.
  - D3: the full chat history is in
    C:\Users\milin\.claude\projects\C--Users-milin-OneDrive-Desktop-Sefer-SWE-As
    signment\c13aca05-9fd7-4af0-82c8-87d87c7604bb.jsonl. The session's context
    was summarized automatically once, but that file still holds every message,
    including the earlier ones. The summary on its own is not the complete
    history.

  Checkpoint C (Git): your approval needed

  What I found, using only commands that change nothing:
  - The Git repository root is your home folder (C:/Users/milin), on branch
    main, with no commits and no remote.
  - That repository can see everything under your home folder, including .ssh/
    and NTUSER.DAT. A careless git add there could stage private files.

  Proposed way to isolate the project:
  1. Run git init -b main inside the project folder. That creates a separate
     repository, and Git commands run from the project folder will use it
     instead of the home one.
  2. Before staging anything, check that git rev-parse --show-toplevel shows the
     project folder.
  3. Review git status with you, then stage files by name. node_modules, dist
     and .env are already excluded by .gitignore.
  4. Make the first commit only after you've reviewed the file list.

  I won't touch your home-folder repository or your global Git settings. Whether
  you want to keep that home-folder repository at all is your call.

  One decision for later, at Checkpoint D: should the published repository
  include Sefer's sample PDFs (sample-docs/)?

  Do you approve Checkpoint C as proposed (a separate repository in the project 
  folder, initial commit after you review the file list)?

  Where they came from: SOURCE.md gives the exact download address for each file

  Licence or permission to share: ❓ none is documented.

    matches are customs data fields such as other-license, not a licence
    statement.
  - The research log and README don't record any terms either.
  - Being publicly downloadable does not prove they may be redistributed.

  What depends on them:
  - The XSD validator loads them every time an analysis runs.
  - If they are missing, analysis still runs, but it says XSD validation did not
    happen, and the sample can't reach "passes local checks".
  - The tests in xml.test.ts, sample.test.ts, workflow.test.ts and
    unseen.test.ts would fail (most of the suite).
  - xsd-spike.mjs also reads them.

  Options:
  - (a) Include them, with SOURCE.md as the credit. Simplest, but there is no
    documented permission.
  - (b) Exclude them and add a script that downloads them and checks the
    fingerprints. This needs a code change and network access for tests, and it
    needs your approval.
  - (c) Ask Sefer.

❯ # Sefer SWE Assignment — Comprehensive Issue Resolution and Quality           
  Improvement                                                                   
                                                                                
  You are the senior software engineer responsible for bringing this take-home  
  assignment to a submission-ready state. Work directly in the existing         
  repository:                                                                   
                                                                                
  `C:\Users\milin\OneDrive\Desktop\Sefer-SWE-Assignment`                        
                                                                                
  The application converts commercial invoices, packing lists, and house bills  
  of lading into a **draft NetCHB-compatible customs entry XML**, identifies    
  missing or conflicting shipment information, and helps users resolve those    
  issues.                                                                       
                                                                                
  Your task is to **inspect the existing implementation, determine exactly what 
  is wrong or incomplete, correct the root causes, and verify every change**.   
  Do not merely produce a report, make cosmetic changes, or suppress errors to  
  make tests pass.                                                              
                                                                                
  ## 1. Critical operating rules                                                
                                                                                
  1. Start by inspecting the repository, Git status, `CLAUDE.md`, `prompt.md`,  
  README, implementation, tests, schemas, generated XML, and validation         
  reports.                                                                      
  2. Treat the existing code and artifacts as the source of truth. Do not       
  assume a previous audit was completely accurate; reproduce important          
  findings.                                                                     
  3. **Do not modify `CLAUDE.md` or `prompt.md`.** Treat them as protected      
  project instructions.                                                         
  4. Preserve the existing architecture, working functionality, UI, and design  
  unless a specific change is needed to fix a demonstrated issue.               
  5. Do not rewrite the application from scratch or introduce unnecessary       
  dependencies.                                                                 
  6. Never invent shipment facts, customs identifiers, HTSUS codes, importer    
  details, port codes, weights, quantities, country of origin, or valuation     
  decisions.                                                                    
  7. Do not turn unresolved information into confirmed data just to satisfy an  
  XSD.                                                                          
  8. Do not delete, overwrite, or weaken existing tests simply because they     
  fail.                                                                         
  9. Do not remove warnings, suppress exceptions, skip validation, loosen       
  schemas, or hardcode success results to create a passing build.               
  10. Do not make network calls to customs systems, submit entries, contact     
  Sefer, or use external inference APIs.                                        
  11. Do not stage, commit, push, publish, or change Git remotes. I will handle 
  GitHub submission separately.                                                 
  12. Work incrementally. Before each major change, identify the problem and    
  intended fix. After changing code, run the relevant tests.                    
  13. Prioritize correctness, auditability, honest handling of uncertainty, and 
  a reliable user experience over unnecessary features.                         
                                                                                
  ## 2. Establish a reproducible baseline                                       
                                                                                
  Before editing:                                                               
                                                                                
  - Confirm the working directory and Git repository ro

  values are handled.                                                           
  - Which schema files are used and their known limitations.                    
  - Why the generated sample is or is not file-ready.                           
  - What has not been tested against real NetCHB/CBP systems.                   
                                                                                
  Keep `docs/approach.md` consistent with the actual implementation and         
  evaluation results. Do not invent performance metrics, test results, or       
  external validation claims.                                                   
                                                                                
  Keep all approved schema/sample files unchanged. Do not publish the

  ## 10. Execution strategy                                                     
                                                                                
  Work in this order:                                                           
                                                                                
  **Phase A — Audit:** reproduce the current state and produce a concise defect 
  list with severity, root cause, affected files, and a proposed fix.           
                                                                                
  **Phase B — Core correctness:** fix data provenance, issue resolution, state  
  persistence, and conflict handling.                                           
                                                                                
  **Phase C — XML and validation:** resolve generator defects, required-field   
  mapping, schema validation, and report consistency.                           
                                                                                
  **Phase D — UI:** fix any problems that prevent the user from resolving       
  issues and regenerating/downloading the draft.                                
                                                                                
  **Phase E — Regression testing:** run typecheck, tests, build, API E2E,

  Do not spend time on cosmetic refactoring when a correctness defect remains.  
  If a task is too risky or unsupported by the source evidence, leave it        
  unresolved, explain why, and identify the exact user/broker decision needed.

  #: D-1
  Severity: High
  Defect: Acknowledging a blocking issue unblocks readiness even when its
  disputed or missing values were never confirmed (reproduced above)
  Root cause: The readiness rule only checks the acknowledged flag
  Fix: Each issue gets a resolution: open, acknowledged (still blocking),
  resolved (every affected value confirmed) or waived (an explicit decision,
  with a note, to leave out optional values only)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-2
  Severity: High (UX)
  Defect: A browser refresh loses all documents and decisions
  Root cause: State lives only in React memory
  Fix: Autosave to browser storage, restore on load (validated), and add a
  "Clear
  saved review" control
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-3
  Severity: Medium
  Defect: Downloads can come from an out-of-date analysis while a new one is
  still running
  Root cause: No pending or out-of-date state in the UI
  Fix: "Updating…" indicator; downloads disabled until the result matches the
  current inputs
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-4
  Severity: Medium
  Defect: The UI, the report and the XML can't be tied to the same input state;
  counts are worked out separately in each place
  Root cause: No shared summary or fingerprint of the inputs
  Fix: The server calculates summary counts plus an inputFingerprint (SHA-256 of

  documents + decisions); the UI, report and XML header all show the same
  values
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-5
  Severity: Medium
  Defect: XSD errors aren't linked to the fields responsible
  Root cause: Errors passed through as plain text
  Fix: Each error is linked to the missing fields it concerns, or flagged as
  unexplained (which would indicate a builder bug)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-6
  Severity: Medium
  Defect: "Withheld because of a conflict" looks the same as "missing"
  Root cause: A single missing label for both
  Fix: New withheld label when a disputed document value is held back
  ────────────────────────────────────────

● I fixed the root causes of eight confirmed defects; the most serious was that
  ticking an issue as reviewed could make an entry pass with disputed facts

  1. Fixed

  #: D-1 (high)
  Defect, reproduced before fixing: With every issue acknowledged and the
  required values typed, the sample reached "passes local checks" while gross
  weight (C-4), container number (C-5), the MIDs and the ultimate consignee's
  tax ID were never confirmed
  Root cause: Readiness treated "acknowledged" as "closed"
  Fix: The server now works out each issue's resolution: open → reviewed, values
   
  still needed (still blocking) → resolved (every affected value confirmed) or
  waived (only for optional values, and only with a note). Reviewing never
  confirms a value
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-2 (high)
  Defect, reproduced before fixing: A browser refresh lost all documents and
  decisions
  Root cause: State lived only in page memory
  Fix: Autosave in the browser, checked against the request format when
  restored,
  plus a "Clear saved review" button
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-3
  Defect, reproduced before fixing: Downloads could come from a result older
  than
  the latest change
  Root cause: No "updating" state
  Fix: "Updating…" indicator; downloads disabled until the result is current
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-4
  Defect, reproduced before fixing: Counts could differ between UI, report and
  XML, and nothing tied them to one input state
  Root cause: Counts calculated in several places
  Fix: The server calculates the counts once, plus a fingerprint of the inputs.
  The UI, report and XML header all show the same values (checked in a real
  browser)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-5
  Defect, reproduced before fixing: XSD errors weren't linked to the fields
  causing them
  Root cause: Raw error text only
  Fix: Each error is traced to the fields responsible. Any error that can't be
  traced is marked "unexplained" as a possible XML-builder bug. All 10 sample
  errors trace to missing data
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-6
  Defect, reproduced before fixing: "Missing" also covered values held back
  because the documents disagree
  Root cause: One label for both cases
  Fix: New withheld label, separate from missing and proposed
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-7
  Defect, reproduced before fixing: C-12 arithmetic not checked; the C-6 amount
  was buried in the evidence
  Root cause: Not implemented
  Fix: Checked: 23,235.00 + 3,850.00 + 185.00 = 27,270.00, which equals the
  printed total. The issue explanation now quotes "value USD 4,180.00" as
  printed. Nothing is added or deducted
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: D-8
  Defect, reproduced before fixing: The report didn't say what was left out of
  the XML, or why
  Root cause: —
  Fix: New "Not written to the XML" section: withheld, proposals awaiting
  confirmation, and missing

  I checked C-1 to C-12 against the documents. All twelve keep both source
  values and stay unresolved until a broker decides. C-5's check digit is
  treated as "supports but does not prove" the handwritten correction.

  Two existing tests changed because the corrected rules require it; no check
  was removed or loosened:
  - The clean synthetic shipment's "complete" input now also supplies the
    ultimate consignee's tax ID. Without it, that issue correctly stays pending.
  - The XML-comment checks now look for the more precise comments, which include
    the field path.

● I'll inspect before reporting, starting with the upload and extraction path,
  how scans are handled, and what the manual-entry UI already offers.

● The scans are low resolution (140-150 dpi vs. the 300 dpi OCR typically

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Your concern is partly right. Users don't strictly need a JSON file: every
  scanned document already has a Start manual entry form for typing values
  directly in the browser. But nothing is read from a scanned PDF automatically,
  so for the sample's packing list and bill of lading someone has to type
  everything (about 30 fields, lines and container readings) or import a
  ready-made file.

  I've inspected the code and haven't changed anything.

  1. What the app can process directly today

  PDF kind: Commercial invoice with a text layer (text you can select)
  What happens: ✅ Read automatically: document type, header fields, parties and

  goods lines, each with page number and exact printed text. Tested on the
  sample's layout and one made-up alternative layout
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  PDF kind: Packing list or bill of lading with a text layer
  What happens: ⚠️ Type recognised from the title and text kept for the
  cross-document checks, but no fields are filled in (no reader for these
  document types yet)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  PDF kind: Scanned PDF, any type (image only, no text layer)
  What happens: ❌ Nothing read. Type guessed from the file name; method shown
  as
  "unavailable"

  2. Why the sample's packing list and bill of lading need a transcription

  - Each is a single page holding one embedded image with no text layer:
    1638×1158 px (about 140 dpi) and 1241×1754 px (about 150 dpi).
  - The app reads only a PDF's text layer and has no OCR (text recognition from
    images). That matched the earlier decision not to use a paid AI or vision
    service.
  - So these documents go through the manual path:
    - Start manual entry opens an in-browser form. No JSON is involved; the user
      types the values.
    - Import transcription JSON is optional. It exists so the sample can be
      reproduced exactly in tests and in D2, and so a typed-up document can be
      saved and reloaded.
  - The two files in fixtures/ are the transcriptions I typed from the images
    during development.

  The README and UI wording put the JSON import first, which makes it look
  required. It isn't.

  3. The smallest safe change (no new packages, about 1 hour)

  1. Show the PDF next to the form. The browser can display the uploaded PDF
     itself in a panel beside the manual-entry form, so users type from what
     they see without switching windows.
  2. Make manual entry the main path. Lead with "Start manual entry", label JSON
     import as "optional: load a saved transcription", and fix the README and
     UI wording.
  3. Keep everything else as it is: the transcription format, the import/export,
     the fingerprint check that binds a transcription to its PDF, and the
     fixtures used by tests and D2.

  This doesn't read scans automatically. It removes the impression that JSON is
  required and makes typing practical.

  4. Can OCR be added with the current tools and time?

  Technically yes; realistically it's risky before 2 PM.

  - What it would need:
    - The PDF library already installed (unpdf) can pull the page image out of a
      scan (extractImages, no extra packages).
    - Node's built-in compression could turn that image into a PNG.
    - Text recognition would need tesseract.js: Apache-2.0 licence, runs as
      WebAssembly with nothing to compile.
    - The English language data would need to be installed locally
      (@tesseract.js-data/eng, roughly 10–20 MB), so the app never downloads
      anything at runtime.
    - That's two new packages, which need your approval under CLAUDE.md §5.2.
  - Expected quality: poor to fair.
    - At about 140–150 dpi, small print and numbers are likely to be misread.
    - The handwritten container correction (C-5) and the rubber stamp won't be
      read reliably.
    - I can't give an accuracy figure without actually trying it.
  - Work beyond the OCR itself:
    - OCR only produces raw text. Turning it into packing-list and
      bill-of-lading fields needs two new label-based readers (like the invoice
      one), built to tolerate OCR errors.
    - Tests are also needed.
    - Estimate: 5–7 hours, against roughly 12 hours left, with the commit, push
      and D3 export still to do.
  - Memory: a recognition worker uses roughly 100–300 MB, on a machine that has
    already run out of memory once today.

  My recommendation: do the no-dependency change in section 3 now. If you want
  OCR, approve a 30–45 minute trial first: install tesseract.js and measure its
  accuracy on the two sample scans, then decide. The trial would also tell us
  whether to stop at "OCR text as a typing aid" (much cheaper than automatic
  field filling).

  5. If OCR is added: keeping evidence and never inventing values

  - New extraction method ocr, separate from "text layer" and "manual".
    - Every value it produces records the page, the exact OCR text as its quote,
      and the OCR confidence in its note.
  - Nothing read by OCR goes into the XML until a person confirms it.
    - Each OCR'd document raises a blocking "OCR values not yet verified" issue,
      which clears only when the user confirms the values (or corrects them in
      the form).
    - This uses the existing review, resolution and waiver rules, so OCR text
      can never pass unchecked.
  - Low-confidence values are left blank: below a set confidence level a field
    stays empty, with the OCR text kept as a note, rather than filled with a
    guess.
  - Handwriting and stamps are never read automatically: corrected or
    overwritten areas (such as the C-5 container number) stay as "Provisional —
    confirmation required".
  - Keyword checks are labelled: OCR text that feeds the keyword checks (assist,
    free-of-charge, origin) is marked as OCR text in the evidence, so a misread
    can't silently change a decision.
  - What stays the same: the transcription workflow and the fingerprint binding
    stay. The D2 sample keeps using the fixed transcriptions, so it stays
    reproducible and isn't affected by OCR changes.

  6. Tests and documentation that would change

  Area: shared/src/documents.ts and shared/test/schemas.test.ts
  Section 3 change: —
  OCR change, in addition: New ocr method; check that it is required to carry
  confidence notes
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: server/test/extraction.test.ts
  Section 3 change: —
  OCR change, in addition: Scans produce OCR text and are marked "unverified";
  low confidence leaves fields blank; nothing is invented. The same engine
  version gives repeatable output, so exact results can be pinned
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Area: server/test/sample.test.ts, workflow.test.ts

  ────────────────────────────────────────

  ────────────────────────────────────────

  confirm them
  ────────────────────────────────────────

  ────────────────────────────────────────

  Not affected: the protected files, the XML builder, validation, the schemas,
  and the D2 files (unless you choose to regenerate D2 from OCR).

  Your decision

  2. Section 3 plus the OCR trial (30–45 minutes, installing tesseract.js and
     the English language data), then decide whether to continue.
  3. Full OCR now (5–7 hours, two new packages, real risk to the deadline).

❯ # Implement Complete PDF-to-NetCHB Entry Workflow                             
                                                                                
  We are changing the current implementation strategy. I do not want a solution 
  where users must manually prepare transcription JSON files for scanned PDFs.  
  Implement a proper end-to-end PDF ingestion and extraction workflow in the    
  existing Sefer SWE take-home repository.                                      
                                                                                
  ## 1. Inspect before editing                                                  
                                                                                
  Inspect the current repository, including:                                    
  - PDF upload API and document classification.                                 
  - Existing PDF text extraction and invoice parser.                            
  - Shared document and evidence types.                                         
  - Manual transcription workflow and fingerprint validation.                   
  - Packing-list and bill-of-lading parsing.                                    
  - Analysis rules, issue resolution, XML builder and XSD validation.           
  - Existing tests, fixtures, README, approach write-up and test report.        
  - Project constraints in CLAUDE.md and prompt.md.                             
                                                                                
  Preserve the existing architecture and working functionality wherever         
  possible. Do not create a separate application or rewrite the XML builder.    
                                                                                
  ## 2. Required user workflow                                                  
                                                                                
  A user must be able to upload the commercial invoice, packing list and bill   
  of lading as PDFs directly through the UI.                                    
                                                                                
  The application must:                                                         
  - Identify whether each PDF has an extractable text layer or contains scanned 
  pages.                                                                        
  - Extract text-layer PDFs without requiring JSON.                             
  - Run local OCR for scanned pages.                                            
  - Parse each document type into structured fields using document-specific,    
  error-tolerant logic.                                                         
  - Display extracted fields, source page, evidence text and extraction method. 
  - Allow the user to correct, confirm or reject extracted values.              
  - Reconcile conflicting values across documents.                              
  - Generate the existing NetCHB XML and validation report from the reviewed    
  data.                                                                         
  - Explain all missing information and broker decisions still required.        
                                                                                
  JSON transcription import may remain as an optional development/testing or    
  saved-transcription feature, but it must not be required for the normal PDF   
  workflow.                                                                     
                                                                                
  ## 3. OCR implementation                                                      
                                                                                
  Prefer local OCR with no external inference API. First check the existing PDF 
  library's capabilities and the compatibility of OCR dependencies with the     
  project's Node version and operating system.                                  
                                                                                
  Use a maintainable OCR integration that:                                      
  - Handles scanned PDF pages.                                                  
  - Processes pages sequentially or with bounded concurrency to control memory. 
  - Has file-size, page-count, execution-time and failure limits.               
  - Does not download language data at runtime.                                 
  - Reports useful errors when OCR cannot run.                                  
  - Does not silently treat OCR failure as an empty but successfully read       
  document.                                                                     
                                                                                
  If OCR requires new dependencies or language data, check licensing,           
  installation requirements and project constraints before proceeding. Do not   
  bypass dependency-approval requirements in CLAUDE.md. If explicit approval is 
  required, report the exact packages and wait for my approval.                 
                                                                                
  ## 4. Extraction and safety requirements                                      
                                                                                
  OCR is not ground truth.                                                      
                                                                                
  - Record extraction method and page number for every extracted value.         
  - Preserve the original OCR/text evidence supporting each value.              
  - Record confidence where the OCR engine supplies it.                         
  - Do not invent values when text is unclear or missing.                       
  - Keep low-confidence readings unconfirmed and request human review.          
  - Never automatically resolve conflicting source documents.                   
  - Never infer handwritten corrections or stamps as certain.                   
  - Do not let unverified OCR values silently enter a ready-to-file XML.        
  - Preserve the existing blocking, acknowledgement, confirmation and waiver    
  rules.                                                                        
  - Acknowledging an issue must not count as confirming an extracted value.     
  - Required schema fields must not be waived.                                  
  - Keep the readiness state NOT READY TO FILE while required decisions or      
  confirmations remain unresolved.                                              
                                                                                
  ## 5. Document-specific parsers                                               
                                                                                
  Implement and test readers for:                                               
  - Commercial invoice: invoice number/date, seller, buyer, consignee when      
  supported, goods descriptions, quantities, unit prices, line totals, subtotal 
  and total.                                                                    
  - Packing list: packages, marks, quantities, net/gross weights, dimensions    
  when available, and goods descriptions.                                       
  - Bill of lading: shipper, consignee, notify party when available, B/L        
  number, vessel/voyage, ports, container numbers, seal numbers, package count  
  and gross weight.                                                             
                                                                                
  Use label-based or layout-aware parsing with tolerant handling of line breaks 
  and OCR errors. Do not claim general support for arbitrary layouts based      
  only on the supplied sample. For unsupported layouts, retain evidence and     
  provide a manual correction path.                                             
                                                                                
  ## 6. User interface                                                          
                                                                                
  Make PDF upload the primary entry point.                                      
                                                                                
  For each uploaded document, show:                                             
  - Document name and detected type.                                            
  - Whether text extraction or OCR was used.                                    
  - Processing state, including failures.                                       
  - Extracted field values and confidence/verification status.                  
  - Original PDF or page preview alongside the fields when practical.           
  - A clear manual correction and confirmation path.                            
                                                                                
  Do not make users create, edit or upload JSON files. Any JSON option must be  
  visibly optional and secondary.                                               
                                                                                
  Show the user when OCR is still running. Prevent stale results from           
  overwriting newer edits. Disable XML/report downloads whenever the current    
  review is stale or unverified blockers remain.                                
                                                                                
  ## 7. Tests                                                                   
                                                                                
  Add unit and integration tests for:                                           
  - Text-layer PDF extraction.                                                  
  - Scanned PDF OCR.                                                            
  - OCR failure and unsupported/corrupt PDFs.                                   
  - OCR confidence and unconfirmed fields.                                      
  - Each document-specific parser.                                              
  - Missing and conflicting data.                                               
  - User confirmation and correction.                                           
  - Blocking issues remaining unresolved until valid confirmation.              
  - XML generation and XSD validation.                                          
  - PDF upload through the browser without any JSON file.                       
  - Existing sample fixtures and reproducible D2 output.                        
                                                                                
  Do not remove assertions, weaken tests or change expected safety behaviour    
  merely to get green tests.                                                    
                                                                                
  ## 8. Deliverables and constraints                                            
                                                                                
  Update README.md, docs/approach.md, docs/test-report.md and relevant research 
  notes so they accurately describe the new implementation and its measured     
  limitations.                                                                  
                                                                                
  Preserve:                                                                     
  - The TypeScript frontend and backend.                                        
  - Existing XML structure and validation.                                      
  - Original source evidence.                                                   
  - D2 sample XML/report consistency.                                           
  - Protected files and official schema files.                                  
  - The full Claude Code conversation history.                                  
                                                                                
  Do not add secrets, use paid/external inference APIs, invent broker data,     
  claim NetCHB/CBP acceptance without testing, or edit protected files without  
  permission.                                                                   
                                                                                
  Do not commit, push, publish or change Git remotes.                           
                                                                                
  ## 9. Execution strategy                                                      
                                                                                
  Work in verified increments:                                                  
  1. Inspect constraints and confirm dependency/licence compatibility.          
  2. Implement OCR for scanned PDFs.                                            
  3. Implement document-specific parsers and evidence tracking.                 
  4. Integrate with existing issue resolution and XML generation.               
  5. Update the UI for direct PDF ingestion.                                    
  6. Run tests and browser E2E sequentially to control memory.                  
  7. Test all three supplied PDFs through the actual upload UI without          
  importing JSON.                                                               
  8. Verify D2 still matches the final reviewed sample.                         
  9. Report exact test counts, commands, limitations, modified files, and any   
  remaining blockers.                                                           
                                                                                
  Prioritize a correct, explainable implementation over superficial completion. 
  If a dependency constraint or technical issue blocks the work, explain it     
  explicitly rather than creating a fake or partially working implementation.

  optional section—while keeping the existing layout intact.
