  What changed

  - OCR: tesseract.js@7.0.0 with the English model @tesseract.js-data/eng@1.0.0,
    the two packages you approved, pinned and installed without scripts.
    - The language data loads from node_modules; nothing is downloaded at
      runtime and no cache files are written.
    - Pages are processed one at a time on a single worker, with at most 5 pages
      per document and 60 s per page.
    - If OCR fails, the document is reported as "not read", with the reason,
      never as an empty successful read.
  - Readers: new packing-list and B/L readers that work from printed labels and
    column layouts. They also give structured fields to text-layer packing lists
    and B/Ls.
  - OCR is never treated as fact:
    - Every OCR value keeps its page, the OCR text it came from and its
      confidence, and starts as unverified. Readings below 50% confidence are
      left empty, with the OCR text kept as evidence.
    - A blocking OCR_UNVERIFIED issue per scanned document keeps every value
      that depends on an unverified reading out of the XML, including
      suggestions built on it.
    - Ticking an issue as reviewed doesn't verify the readings. Handwriting and
      strike-throughs are never treated as read.
  - UI:
    - PDF upload is the main path, with a message while OCR runs.
    - Each OCR value shows its confidence and status, with Confirm, correct by
      typing, Reject, and a "confirm all" that asks first.
    - There's a page preview toggle, and downloads are disabled while OCR
      readings are unverified.
    - Transcription JSON is now an optional, collapsed section.

  Measured OCR accuracy on the sample scans

  ┌──────────┬───────┬─────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
  │ Document │ Exact │   Wrong (left unverified)   │       Not read       │
  ├──────────┼───────┼─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ Packing  │ 16 of │                             │ 2: the shipper (3%   │
  │ list     │  18   │ 0                           │ confidence) and the  │
  │          │       │                             │ shipping marks       │
  ├──────────┼───────┼─────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │          │       │ 5: "WN" for VN, "DODR" for  │                      │
  │ B/L      │ 17 of │ DOOR, "24 SEP 206", and     │ 3: master B/L,       │
  │          │  25   │ partial HS codes and        │ notify party, marks  │
  │          │       │ freight terms               │                      │
  └──────────┴───────┴─────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘

  The struck-through container number came out as BRLU3841728 at 0% confidence
  and wasn't used; the handwritten correction isn't read.

  XML status

  The D2 sample is NOT_READY_TO_FILE:
  - Blocking issues: 16 issues, of which 15 are blocking and all 15 unresolved.
  - Required/conditional field checks: 32 of 41 failed.
  - XSD errors: 10, all traced to missing broker data.

  It has no <transmit>. D2 is still built from the reviewed transcriptions,
  which stand for what you'd have after checking every OCR reading, so it stays
  reproducible.

  Limitations

  - Goods table: the packing list's goods table isn't read by OCR, so its lines
    must be typed if you want the per-style quantity and fibre checks (C-2/C-3).
  - Handwriting: handwriting and stamps aren't read.
  - Layouts: the readers are proven only on the sample layouts and one made-up
    layout each.
  - Language and scans: English model only, and no real scanned invoice was
    tested.
  - Page preview: I couldn't check how it looks, because headless Chrome shows
    the PDF area blank. Please check it in a normal browser.
  - No acceptance testing: nothing was tested against NetCHB or CBP.

  You are working in the existing Sefer SWE take-home assignment repository.    
  This is the final implementation and delivery phase. The goal is to deliver a 
  reliable, tested, evidence-based shipment entry drafting application using    
  the three provided sample PDFs: commercial invoice, packing list, and bill of 
  lading.                                                                       
                                                                                
  ## 1. Non-negotiable rules                                                    
                                                                                
  - Inspect the existing repository, `CLAUDE.md`, `prompt.md`, README,          
  architecture, current tests, and latest test report before modifying          
  anything.                                                                     
  - Do not rewrite the application from scratch.                                
  - Do not edit `CLAUDE.md` or `prompt.md`.                                     
  - Preserve all official NetCHB XSD files unchanged. Do not invent schema      
  elements or alter their semantics.                                            
  - Keep the application local-first; do not introduce external inference APIs, 
  unnecessary dependencies, or external uploads of shipment documents.          
  - Do not invent importer details, tax IDs, entry numbers, tariff              
  classifications, tariff values, broker decisions, or document facts.          
  - Preserve original extracted values and their source evidence when proposing 
  a normalized value.                                                           
  - Do not claim that XML is accepted by NetCHB or CBP. We only have local      
  validation evidence.                                                          
  - Do not stage, commit, push, publish, or create a remote without my explicit 
  approval.                                                                     
  - Preserve the existing complete Claude Code conversation history. Never      
  truncate, delete, or rewrite it.                                              
                                                                                
  ## 2. First audit the current implementation                                  
                                                                                
  Inspect the existing extraction, OCR, field mapping, cross-document           
  validation, broker input, issue resolution, XML builder, XSD validation,      
  frontend state management, persistence, and download logic.                   
                                                                                
  Run the existing test suite before changes and record the baseline. Identify  
  the smallest changes that will materially reduce manual data entry without    
  compromising customs-data accuracy.                                           
                                                                                
  The current XML contains missing entry/header fields, unresolved gross-weight 
  and carrier-code issues, missing or unconfirmed bill-of-lading fields, an     
  unapproved invoice-number transformation, missing tariff numbers and tariff   
  values for five lines, and an unresolved origin for the sample line. Use the  
  current repository and validation report to establish the authoritative issue 
  list rather than hardcoding this list.                                        
                                                                                
  ## 3. Improve the extraction-to-entry workflow                                
                                                                                
  Build on the current three-document upload and OCR review functionality.      
                                                                                
  For every field, maintain or derive:                                          
  - Extracted/proposed value.                                                   
  - Source document and available page or text evidence.                        
  - Extraction method and confidence, where available.                          
  - Whether the value is unverified, user-confirmed, broker-confirmed, or       
  derived.                                                                      
  - Whether it conflicts with another document.                                 
  - Whether it is eligible to be included in XML.                               
                                                                                
  Avoid duplicating state unnecessarily. Follow the existing architecture and   
  use one authoritative data model.                                             
                                                                                
  Automatically populate fields from reliable extracted evidence. Reuse a       
  verified fact across fields only when the relationship is valid; do not       
  assume that similar field names mean the facts are interchangeable.           
                                                                                
  Use deterministic parsing and calculations wherever possible. Do not add an   
  LLM merely to make the demo look more automated.                              
                                                                                
  ## 4. Add or improve a guided review queue                                    
                                                                                
  Instead of requiring the user to navigate many separate fields, provide a     
  compact review experience grouped into:                                       
                                                                                
  A. Confirm suggested values.                                                  
  B. Resolve conflicting documents.                                             
  C. Supply missing broker/importer information.                                
  D. Resolve tariff classification and valuation.                               
  E. Review final XML validation.                                               
                                                                                
  Each item should show the field, proposed value, evidence/source, confidence  
  where available, why confirmation is needed, and the action required.         
                                                                                
  Support efficient actions such as:                                            
  - Confirm a supported suggestion.                                             
  - Correct a value.                                                            
  - Choose between conflicting source values with an explicit resolution note   
  when appropriate.                                                             
  - Mark a field as needing broker input.                                       
  - Apply a permitted optional-field waiver with a reason, where the existing   
  validation rules allow it.                                                    
                                                                                
  A bulk-confirm action is allowed only for clearly presented, individually     
  reviewable suggestions. It must not silently accept low-confidence OCR,       
  conflicting values, or unsupported inferences.                                
                                                                                
  Never allow an acknowledgement alone to clear a blocking issue. Do not allow  
  waivers for schema-required fields.                                           
                                                                                
  ## 5. Improve cross-document reconciliation                                   
                                                                                
  Use actual extracted document evidence to compare relevant fields, including: 
  - Invoice and packing-list quantities.                                        
  - Fibre/material composition.                                                 
  - Country of origin.                                                          
  - Gross weight.                                                               
  - Container number and handwritten corrections.                               
  - Invoice totals and line-item arithmetic.                                    
  - Invoice number normalization.                                               
  - Shipping parties and bill-of-lading identifiers.                            
                                                                                
  Display the source values side by side. Explain the discrepancy and what      
  evidence or decision is needed. Where a deterministic arithmetic check is     
  possible, calculate it automatically and show the calculation.                
                                                                                
  Do not select a winner based solely on confidence or document type when the   
  discrepancy requires human or broker judgment. Never silently overwrite the   
  original value.                                                               
                                                                                
  ## 6. Reduce repetitive broker input safely                                   
                                                                                
  Identify genuinely reusable verified data and avoid asking for the same fact  
  repeatedly. If the same confirmed value applies to multiple lines, support a  
  single explicit action to apply it to those lines where semantically valid.   
                                                                                
  Clearly distinguish:                                                          
  - Document-derived facts.                                                     
  - Deterministically derived values.                                           
  - Proposed mappings requiring approval.                                       
  - Broker-only information.                                                    
  - Missing mandatory information.                                              
                                                                                
  Do not invent HTSUS digits, MID codes, port mappings, importer identifiers,   
  valuation adjustments, or entry settings to reduce the blocker count.         
                                                                                
  Investigate whether every blocking issue is a true data gap, a conflict, a    
  mapping limitation, or a software defect. Fix software defects; do not        
  disguise data gaps as resolved issues.                                        
                                                                                
  ## 7. Ensure XML and validation always stay synchronized                      
                                                                                
  After every edit, confirmation, correction, resolution, or waiver:            
  - Recompute the current validation report.                                    
  - Rebuild the XML from the current authoritative state.                       
  - Ensure the XML preview and downloadable XML match.                          
  - Ensure stale asynchronous analysis cannot overwrite newer decisions.        
  - Ensure saved-state restoration revalidates data.                            
  - Keep missing or unresolved values explicitly marked and excluded where the  
  current design requires it.                                                   
  - Preserve a clear NOT READY TO FILE status whenever required information     
  remains unresolved.                                                           
                                                                                
  If any field appears in the UI but is not reflected in XML, fix the mapping   
  and add a regression test.                                                    
                                                                                
  Do not introduce a bypass that enables downloading a file presented as ready  
  when mandatory blockers remain.                                               
                                                                                
  ## 8. Test with all three actual PDFs                                         
                                                                                
  Use the existing three sample PDFs, not only synthetic data.                  
                                                                                
  Verify:                                                                       
  - All three documents upload successfully.                                    
  - Text extraction and local OCR behave as designed.                           
  - OCR uncertainty remains visible.                                            
  - The review queue reflects the actual extracted data.                        
  - Conflicts are detected and cannot be silently accepted.                     
  - Editing a value updates the generated XML.                                  
  - Correcting a field removes only the corresponding resolved issue.           
  - Mandatory missing information remains blocking.                             
  - Optional waivers work only when allowed and documented.                     
  - Refreshing or restoring saved state does not lose or incorrectly trust      
  decisions.                                                                    
  - XML validation and download gating remain consistent.                       
  - No stale draft, duplicate lines, or stale validation result appears after   
  edits.                                                                        
                                                                                
  Add regression tests for any defects discovered. Run typecheck, unit tests,   
  build, API E2E, and browser E2E using the repository's established scripts.   
  Report actual pass/fail results; do not claim a test passed unless it ran     
  successfully.                                                                 
                                                                                
  Manually inspect the running UI, including PDF preview, document review,      
  entry fields, broker input, issue queue, XML preview, and download behavior.  
  If a check cannot be performed, explicitly report the limitation.             
                                                                                
  ## 9. Final deliverables and repository hygiene                               
                                                                                
  Review and finalize:                                                          
  - Working TypeScript frontend and backend.                                    
  - README with setup, commands, architecture, limitations, and sample          
  workflow.                                                                     
  - One-page approach write-up.                                                 
  - Test report with actual results and known limitations.                      
  - Research log documenting official NetCHB sources, schema/sample             
  discrepancies, and what was and was not verified.                             
  - Generated sample XML and its validation report, consistent with the final   
  code and actual review state.                                                 
  - Complete required AI conversation history, preserved without truncation.    
  - A clear explanation of unresolved customs facts and why they must not be    
  guessed.                                                                      
                                                                                
  Do not include secrets, unnecessary caches, temporary files, local            
  environment files, or personal machine-specific paths. Check dependency       
  lockfiles and repository contents.                                            
                                                                                
  Inspect whether the sample PDFs and official schema/sample files may legally  
  be redistributed before making the repository public. Keep the repository     
  private unless publication is explicitly approved and permissions have been   
  considered.                                                                   
                                                                                
  ## 10. Final report                                                           
                                                                                
  When implementation and verification are complete, provide:                   
  1. Summary of changes.                                                        
  2. Files changed and why.                                                     
  3. Before/after manual-entry improvements.                                    
  4. Test commands and actual results.                                          
  5. Results for each of the three PDFs.                                        
  6. Current unresolved blockers and the exact information needed to resolve    
  each.                                                                         
  7. Whether the XML is technically valid against the local schema and whether  
  the draft is ready to file, reported as two separate outcomes.                
  8. Remaining limitations.                                                     
  9. Exact Git status, branch, configured remote status, and deliverables       
  checklist.                                                                    
                                                                                
  Stop before staging, committing, pushing, or making the repository public.    
  Ask for explicit approval before those operations.

● The improved PDF-to-entry workflow is finished and verified, but the sample
  draft is still not ready to file. Its XML also fails the local schema check:
  10 errors, every one caused by information that is genuinely missing. Nothing
  was staged, committed or pushed.

  The main change is a new Review queue tab (A–E). It shows the open work on one
  page and records decisions through the existing broker-input model, so there
  is still one source of truth and the XML is rebuilt after every action.

  1. Summary of changes

  - A. Confirm suggestions: each suggestion shows its reason and evidence. The
    same suggestion on several lines is one row with one button. "Confirm all…"
    asks first and never includes conflicts or unverified OCR readings.
    Confirmations can be undone.
  - B. Resolve conflicts: the sources are shown side by side with OCR
    confidence. Each source's value is offered already converted to the field's
    format (for example 1,888.00 KGS becomes 1888). "Use this value" stays
    disabled until a decision note is written, and nothing is chosen
    automatically.
  - C. Broker and importer data: the entry-number option and every missing
    broker field. A typed line value (such as an MID) can be applied to all
    lines in one action, after a confirmation prompt. Fields can be marked
    "Waiting for broker"; these are listed in the report and fill nothing.
  - D. Tariff and valuation: per line, the invoice's HS digits, amount and
    quantity are shown as evidence only, next to inputs for the 10-digit HTSUS
    number and the value, plus the valuation issues.
  - E. Final validation: status, unresolved blockers, failed checks, XSD errors.
  - Rules that still hold: recording a decision never fills a value,
    schema-required fields can't be waived, and a restored review is re-analysed
    rather than trusted.

  Defects found by driving the real PDFs through the queue (all fixed, with 
  tests):
  1. Silent skip: on the PDF-only path, the invoice-vs-packing-list quantity and
     fibre checks were skipped silently, because OCR doesn't read the packing
     list's goods table. That table's OCR text drops style codes and quantities,
     so I didn't build a reader on it. Instead, a new blocking issue now says
     "Invoice lines could not be compared with the packing list".
  2. "null (unknown)": an unreadable container number was described as "null
     (unknown)". It now says "no reliable reading (source text: …)".
  3. Outdated upload hint: it said scanned PDFs can't be read.
  4. Outdated fixture note: the sample transcriptions said "no OCR configured",
     which also appeared in the sample report. I reworded the note; the values
     are unchanged.

❯ how do I export the D3 chat history?                                          

● You have two ways, and I'd submit both: a readable export, and the raw log
  file, which is the complete record.

  1. Readable export with /export

  Type this at the Claude Code prompt:

  /export D3-chat-history.txt

  That saves the conversation as a plain-text file in the current folder. If you
  type /export with no filename, it asks whether to copy the conversation to
  the clipboard or save it to a file.

  This conversation's context was summarised automatically several times. A text
  export may therefore show those summaries instead of every earlier message
  word for word, which is why the second file matters.

  2. The complete raw log (JSONL)

  Claude Code writes every message and tool call to this file, including
  everything from before the summaries:

  C:\Users\milin\.claude\projects\C--Users-milin-OneDrive-Desktop-Sefer-SWE-Assi
  gnment\c13aca05-9fd7-4af0-82c8-87d87c7604bb.jsonl

  Copy it rather than moving it, so the original stays where it is, for example:

  ! copy "C:\Users\milin\.claude\projects\C--Users-milin-OneDrive-Desktop-Sefer-
  SWE-Assignment\c13aca05-9fd7-4af0-82c8-87d87c7604bb.jsonl" 
  "%USERPROFILE%\Desktop\D3-chat-history.jsonl"

  Timing. Export at the very end, after your last message, so the file includes
  everything. Anything said after the export won't be in it.

  Before you submit or commit it:
  - Check what's inside. The log contains local file paths, your username, your
    email address (the one Claude Code knows you by) and the full text of every
    command and tool result. Skim it before sharing.
  - Choose where it goes. Your README describes D3 as "exported separately", so
    you can send it alongside the GitHub link. If you'd rather put it in the
    repository (say, under docs/), that's a new commit and push. Tell me and
    I'll review the file for secrets first.

● I've fixed it: on the PDF-only path the packing-list goods table now reads 5
  of 5 rows, and the invoice lines are compared with them. Nothing is committed
  or pushed. Typecheck, unit tests, build, API e2e and browser e2e all pass.

  Root cause

  It was neither OCR quality nor PDF text extraction, and not a hand-off between
  stages either. It came from how the table is laid out, plus a missing reader:

  1. The OCR layout step dropped the table's number cells. I dumped the OCR
     words with their positions and cropped the page image. The numbers are
     printed crisply, but each sits alone in a ruled cell, and tesseract's
     layout step discarded most of them as graphics. For NB-T101 it returned
     only 10.00 and 480.00 of the seven printed numbers. Style codes,
     descriptions and fibre lines were read fine. The PDF has no text layer, so
     OCR is the only source.
  2. The packing-list reader had no row reader at all. It always returned zero
     rows with a fixed warning.

  Changing tesseract's page-segmentation mode alone (modes 4, 6 and 11) did not
  recover the cells. Erasing the ruling lines first did.

  The fix

  - Second OCR pass for packing lists. Long straight dark runs (30 pt or more,
    i.e. the table lines) are erased, then the page is read again. It uses the
    same local tesseract.js, with no new dependency and no external API.
  - New goods-table reader. It accepts a row only where cartons × pieces per
    carton = total pieces among the numbers read. Weights are kept only when
    both the net and the gross pair check out. So a misread digit or a stray
    mark can't become a quantity.
  - Evidence kept. Every cell keeps its page, quote and confidence and stays
    unverified. Readings below 50% confidence are withheld, and nothing reaches
    the XML until a person confirms it.
  - Gaps are reported, not skipped. Three new blocking reports, so a missing
    check is never silent:
    - QUANTITY_NOT_COMPARED when a matched row has no readable quantity. That
      check used to be skipped silently, which could also happen with manually
      entered rows.
    - PACKING_LIST_ROWS_TOTAL when the rows don't add up to the packing list's
      grand total.
    - STYLE_NOT_ON_PACKING_LIST now points at any row whose style couldn't be
      read.
  - Fallback kept. If no row can be read, the blocking warning remains and Add 
    line is still the manual route.

  Before and after (real sample scan)

  Goods rows
  Before: 0
  After: 5 of 5: quantities 2,400 / 1,176 / 900 / 600 / 24 and the two-line
  descriptions match the transcription
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Withheld honestly
  Before: –
  After: W-B220's style (47% confidence); that row's weights (9.60 was misread
  as
  960, so the check rejected them)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Issues on the PDF-only path
  Before: PACKING_LIST_ROWS_NOT_READ
  After: NB-H205 quantity (1,200 vs 1,176) and fibre (60/40 vs 65/35) conflicts,

  the same as the reviewed-transcription path

  Once the unreadable style is entered, all five invoice lines are compared; a
  test covers this. The submitted sample XML (D2) is unchanged, because it is
  built from the reviewed transcriptions.

  Files changed

  - New:
    - server/src/extraction/packingListTable.ts (the reader)
    - server/test/packingTable.test.ts (13 tests)
  - Changed:
    - server/src/extraction/ocr.ts: line removal and the table pass
    - server/src/extraction/index.ts: runs the second pass for packing lists
    - server/src/extraction/documentParsers.ts: returns the rows and warnings
    - server/src/extraction/pdfText.ts: keeps each word's right edge
    - server/src/analysis/rules.ts: the three rule changes above
    - server/test/ocr.test.ts
    - scripts/e2e/browser-e2e.mjs
    - README.md, docs/approach.md, docs/test-report.md
